=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is a sequence A = (A_1,\dots,A_N). Determine whether there are at least two subsequences of A that match the sequence B = (B_1,\dots,B_M). Two subsequences are distinguished if they are taken from different positions, even if they coincide as sequences.

Subsequence
A subsequence of A is a sequence obtained by removing zero or more elements from A and leaving the remaining elements in their original order.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M
A_1 A_2 \ldots A_N
B_1 B_2 \ldots B_M

Output

If there are at least two subsequences of A that match B, print Yes. Otherwise, print No.

Constraints


- 1 \leq M \leq N \leq 2\times 10^5
- 1 \leq A_i \leq 10^9
- 1 \leq B_i \leq 10^9
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

4 2
1 2 1 2
1 2

Sample Output 1

Yes

There are three subsequences of A that match B: (A_1,A_2), (A_1,A_4), (A_3,A_4).

Sample Input 2

3 2
1 2 1
1 2

Sample Output 2

No

There is only one subsequence of A that matches B: (A_1,A_2).

Sample Input 3

3 2
1 1 2
2 1

Sample Output 3

No

There are no subsequences of A that match B.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Implementation:

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N, M = data[0], data[1]
    A = data[2:2+N]
    B = data[2+N:2+N+M]
    left = [-1]*M
    j = 0
    for i, x in enumerate(A):
        if j < M and x == B[j]:
            left[j] = i
            j += 1
            if j == M:
                break
    if j < M:
        print("No")
        return
    right = [-1]*M
    j = M-1
    for i in range(N-1, -1, -1):
        if j >= 0 and A[i] == B[j]:
            right[j] = i
            j -= 1
            if j < 0:
                break
    if j >= 0:
        # Should not happen if left exists? But maybe due to bug; output No.
        print("No")
        return
    for k in range(M):
        if left[k] != right[k]:
            print("Yes")
            return
    print("No")

if __name__ == "__main__": main()

Code:

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    M = data[1]
    A_start = 2
    B_start = 2 + N

    left = [-1] * M
    j = 0
    for i in range(N):
        if j < M and data[A_start + i] == data[B_start + j]:
            left[j] = i
            j += 1
            if j == M:
                break
    if j < M:
        print("No")
        return

    right = [-1] * M
    j = M - 1
    for i in range(N - 1, -1, -1):
        if j >= 0 and data[A_start + i] == data[B_start + j]:
            right[j] = i
            j -= 1
            if j < 0:
                break
    if j >= 0:
        print("No")
        return

    for k in range(M):
        if left[k] != right[k]:
            print("Yes")
            return
    print("No")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Now, let's maybe consider if there is a possible issue with "No" if left and right same but there are two subsequences that are not comparable due to one having earlier p1 but later p2? We proved all occurrences coordinatewise between left and right. Let's test with pattern length 2: left (first a, first b after), right (last b, last a before last b). Suppose there are two occurrences: (a1,b1) and (a2,b2) with a1<a2 but b1>b2? Is that possible? Need a1<a2<b2<b1? Wait b1 is first b after a1; b2 is b after a2. If b1 > b2, then b2 is a b after a1 (since a2>a1 and b2>a2) and before b1, contradicting b1 first b after a1. So no. Thus all occurrences have b order same as a order. For longer, similar. So coordinatewise total order. Good.

Similarly choosing earliest B1 gives more room for later elements. Good.

If no B_{M-1} before last B_M, then no B_{M-1} before any earlier B_M either, because earlier B_M is even earlier and has fewer positions before it. So no. Thus right greedy succeeds if any occurrence. Good.

Could there be a case where choosing last B_M leaves no B_{M-1} before it, but another occurrence with earlier B_M would work? If no B_{M-1} before last B_M, then no B_{M-1} before any earlier B_M either, because earlier B_M is even earlier and has fewer positions before it. So no. Thus right greedy succeeds if any occurrence. Good.

B=[1,2,1]. right j=2 value1: last 1 index3; j=1 value2: last 2 before3 index2; j=0 value1: last 1 before2 index1. succeeds. Good.

Could there be a case where choosing last B_M leaves no B_{M-1} before it, but another occurrence with earlier B_M would work? If no B_{M-1} before last B_M, then no B_{M-1} before any earlier B_M either, because earlier B_M is even earlier and has fewer positions before it. So no. Thus right greedy succeeds if any occurrence. Good.

Now, let's maybe think if there is a possible issue with "left" if choosing earliest B1 leaves no B2 after it, but another occurrence with later B1 would work? If no B2 after earliest B1, then no B2 after later B1. So no. Good.

Now, let's maybe think if there is a possible issue with "subsequence" if two subsequences are distinguished by positions, but if A has equal elements, different positions can produce same sequence; we count them. Good.

No, positions lists are ordered; if any coordinate differs, sets differ. If left and right lists same, positions same.

If no B_{M-1} before last B_M, then no B_{M-1} before any earlier B_M either, because earlier B_M is even earlier and has fewer positions before it. So no. Let's maybe consider if there is a possible issue with "left" and "right" if B appears but left and right same, output No. Suppose B appears exactly once but left and right same. Good. Suppose B appears zero times but left fail. Good.

Now, let's maybe think if there is a possible issue with "at least two subsequences" if B appears multiple times but left and right same? impossible. Good.

Let's maybe think if there is a possible issue with "subsequence" if A has length N and B length M, and there are at least two subsequences but left and right differ only because of positions that are not in increasing order? no.

Now, let's maybe think if there is a possible issue with "right" if we store right and compare after complete; if left exists, right complete. If left doesn't exist, return before right. Good.

Let's maybe think if there is a possible issue with "No" if left exists but right scan fails due to a bug in proof? Let's try to find a counterexample where B appears but right greedy fails. Suppose A=[1,2,1], B=[1,2]? right succeeds. A=[1,2,1,2], B=[1,2,1]? right: last1 index2, last2 before2 index1, last1 before1 index0, succeeds. A=[1,2,1,2,1], B=[1,2,1]? right: last1 index4, last2 before4 index3, last1 before3 index2, succeeds. A=[2,1,2,1,2], B=[1,2,1]? left: first1 index1, first2 after index2, first1 after index3 -> success. right: last1 index3? Wait A indices 0=2,1=1,2=2,3=1,4=2. B=[1,2,1]. right j=2 value1: last 1 index3; j=1 value2: last 2 before3 index2; j=0 value1: last 1 before2 index1. succeeds. Good.

Could there be a case where choosing last B_M leaves no B_{M-1} before it, but another occurrence with earlier B_M would work? If no B_{M-1} before last B_M, then no B_{M-1} before any earlier B_M either, because earlier B_M is even earlier and has fewer positions before it. So no. Thus right greedy succeeds if any occurrence. Good.

Now, let's maybe think if there is a possible issue with "left" if choosing earliest B1 leaves no B2 after it, but another occurrence with later B1 would work? If no B2 after earliest B1, then no B2 after later B1. So no. Good.

Now, let's maybe think if there is a possible issue with "at least two subsequences" if B appears multiple times but left and right same due to all occurrences same positions? impossible.

Now, let's maybe think if there is a possible issue with "subsequence" if two subsequences are distinguished by positions, but if A has equal elements, different positions can produce same sequence; we count them. Good.

Now, let's maybe think if there is a possible issue with "B" values maybe not unique, and left/right positions differ but maybe left and right are same set of positions but assigned to different B indices? No, positions lists are ordered; if any coordinate differs, sets differ. If left and right lists same, positions same.

Now, let's maybe think if there is a possible issue with "No" if left and right same but there are two subsequences because you can choose different positions for equal values but left/right same? If left/right same, no other positions. Good.

Let's maybe think if there is a possible issue with "input values are integers" no.

Now, let's maybe think if there is a possible issue with "N M" if N and M are large and using `data = list(map(int, ...))` might be slower but O(N) fine. 4e5 ints, trivial.

Now, let's maybe think if there is a possible issue with "time limit" maybe 2 seconds, Python O(N) fine.

Now, let's maybe think if there is a possible issue with "right" list if we compare after complete but if left and right differ, output Yes. Good.

Let's maybe decide final code with data list and right list. It is clear.

Let's maybe consider if there is a possible issue with "left" and "right" if B appears but left and right same, output No. Suppose B appears exactly once but left and right same. Good. Suppose B appears zero times but left fail. Good.

Now, let's maybe think if there is a possible issue with "at least two subsequences" if B appears multiple times but left and right same? impossible. Good.

Let's maybe think if there is a possible issue with "subsequence" if A has length N and B length M, and there are at least two subsequences but left and right differ only because of positions that are not in increasing order? no.

Now, let's maybe think if there is a possible issue with "right" if we store right and compare after complete; if left exists, right complete. If left doesn't exist, return before right. Good.

Let's maybe think if there is a possible issue with "No" if left exists but right scan fails due to a bug in proof? Let's try to find a counterexample where B appears but right greedy fails. Suppose A=[1,2,1], B=[1,2]? right succeeds. A=[1,2,1,2], B=[1,2,1]? right: last1 index2, last2 before2 index1, last1 before1 index0, succeeds. A=[1,2,1,2,1], B=[1,2,1]? right: last1 index4, last2 before4 index3, last1 before3 index2, succeeds. A=[2,1,2,1,2], B=[1,2,1]? left: first1 index1, first2 after index2, first1 after index3 -> success. right: last1 index3? Wait A indices 0=2,1=1,2=2,3=1,4=2. B=[1,2,1]. right j=2 value1
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    N, M = data[0], data[1]
    A_start = 2
    B_start = 2 + N

    left = [-1] * M
    j = 0
    for i in range(N):
        if j < M and data[A_start + i] == data[B_start + j]:
            left[j] = i
            j += 1
            if j == M:
                break

    if j < M:
        print("No")
        return

    right = [-1] * M
    j = M - 1
    for i in range(N - 1, -1, -1):
        if j >= 0 and data[A_start + i] == data[B_start + j]:
            right[j] = i
            j -= 1
            if j < 0:
                break

    if j >= 0:
        print("No")
        return

    for k in range(M):
        if left[k] != right[k]:
            print("Yes")
            return

    print("No")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```
